STRICT IMPLEMENTATION CONTRACT — READ THIS FIRST, BEFORE
ANYTHING ELSE
The owner is giving you this prompt because the last round was NOT done properly. In the v16 round you worked from the
WRONG list (the v15, 227-item extraction, not the v16 list) and fixed only 2 items (172/173), then reported NOT READY.
That must never happen again. This contract is binding. If you cannot meet it, say so in your first line — do not silently do
half the job.
1. THE ATTACHED v20 PDF (298 items) IS THE ONLY CHECKLIST. Do not use any other list, extraction, text file, or
   older version. FIRST: confirm you have the v20 PDF, count its items (298), and write the count in your reproduction log.
   If the count is not 298, STOP and say so — do not proceed on a wrong list. The v19 list (287 items) and every earlier
   version are superseded. The 11 new Round 11 items are IDs 292–302; the v18-retest items are IDs 287–291; the
   Round 10 items are IDs 282–286; the v17 sweep items are IDs 274–281. If any of those are missing from the file you
   have, you have the wrong file.
2. COMPLETE implementation, not partial. Every item must be FULLY fixed. A fix is COMPLETE only when ALL of
   these hold: (a) the code change is made, (b) a regression test or a controlled browser check proves the exact
   acceptance condition stated in the list, (c) for a flaky item, the check passes on TWO consecutive runs. "I changed the
   code" is not a fix. "It worked once on my machine" is not a fix for a flaky item. A half-fixed item counts as NOT fixed. If
   you fix the code but cannot prove it with the stated acceptance condition, the item stays open in your report with the
   exact reason.
3. Work the ENTIRE list. All 298 items, in phase order. Do not stop after the P0s. Do not stop after a few P1s. Do not
   send an interim report. The list is not done until every open item is fixed and verified. The P2s are not optional — 137 of
   them are in this list and every one needs a fix or a stated blocker.
4. NO skipping. "Not reproduced" is not a reason to skip an item (standing rule) — fix the code-level cause and verify with
   a controlled/local browser test. If an item is genuinely owner-only (rotation, real payment), mark it as such in the final
   report with the exact blocker — do not silently skip it. If an item needs an owner decision, write the decision question in
   your report — do not skip the item and do not guess the answer.
5. PER-ITEM PROOF. The FINAL-REPORT.md must contain a per-ID table for ALL 298 items: fixed / verified / blocked,
   with the exact proof for each (test name + result, or browser check + evidence). No item may be marked fixed without
   proof. An item with no proof row is an item you did not do.
6. NO interim replies, NO HOLD verdict (standing instruction from Muhammad, 2026-11-14). Report only at the end with
   FINAL-REPORT.md: READY, or the exact owner-only blockers. Do not reply after Phase -1. Do not reply after the P0s.
   Do not reply to say you are stuck — figure it out from the code, the live site, and the 298-item list.
7. REGRESSION GATE. The full suite must pass twice (baseline 465 passed, 2 warnings, TWICE on 66cd0cd — Muse-
   verified; a lower count is a regression). The 77 do-not-regress items must all still pass. The v18 44-tool sweep proved
   42 tools VERIFIED (PDF to PDF/A failed, Translate PARTIAL, PDF to PowerPoint not tested) and Round 11's 33-tool ~5
   MB matrix passed 23 clean — do not regress any of them. If your change breaks a verified_fixed item, it goes back to
   open in your report with the new evidence, and you fix it before reporting.
8. FLAKY items (1, 4, 207) need TWO consecutive clean passes. A single pass is not proof. For ID 1, BOTH languages
   (Spanish AND Hindi) must work cleanly; for ID 207, the 60 MB merge must complete twice; for ID 4, BOTH paths (Drive

THE ENGINEERING LOOP — HOW YOU MUST WORK (the owner's explicit
instruction)
This is not a one-pass fix list. You work in a LOOP, and you do not report until YOU are genuinely satisfied. The owner said
it plainly, three times, the last one the operative one: work like an agent, not a chat model — analyse everything, fix by
debugging, self-analyse after fixing, and keep looping until HE is himself really satisfied; no reply until the app is 100%
(100000%) deployment-ready and 1000% verified by tests + browser tests; no interim reports, no HOLD verdict, no
technical questions — figure it out from the code, the live site, and the 298-item list. "Not reproduced" is not a reason to
skip an item — fix the code-level causes and verify with controlled/local browser tests. The live retest happens after the
owner deploys; your job is to prove the fixes locally first. This instruction SUPERSEDES the v14 prompt's Phase 6 READY-
or-HOLD rule. Here is the loop:

1. ANALYSE (Phase -1). Reproduce each P0 before touching it. Write the reproduction log. Do not fix what you have not
   reproduced.
2. FIX (Phases 0–4). Fix every item, one by one, citing its ID. Add a regression test for every P0 and P1. Implement each
   thing properly — no partial fixes, no fixes that only work on one input or one language, no "fixed" without evidence. A fix
   is complete only when the STRICT CONTRACT's rule 2 holds (code change + proof + two consecutive runs for flaky
   items).
3. SELF-ANALYSE (Phase 5). After fixing, analyse YOUR OWN work as if you were the auditor. Re-run the reproduction
   for every P0 and P1 you fixed. Re-test every fix. Re-run the full test suite twice. Re-check the do-not-regress list (77
   items). Re-verify the contested items. Hunt for: fixes that are incomplete, fixes that only work on one input or one
   language, fixes that introduced a regression, fixes that pass the test but fail the real user flow, and items you skipped or
   marked fixed without evidence.
4. LOOP. If your self-analysis finds ANY gap, regression, incomplete fix, or unaddressed item — go back to step 2, fix it
   properly, and self-analyse again. Repeat the loop. Do NOT stop at the first pass. Do NOT report after the first fix round.
   Do NOT mark an item fixed on a single run — verify each fix twice (two consecutive runs).
5. ONLY WHEN YOU ARE REALLY SATISFIED — when your self-analysis finds nothing left, every P0 is fixed with
   evidence, the suite passes twice, and the do-not-regress list is clean — only then write FINAL-REPORT.md and report
   READY. If you are not satisfied, keep looping. The report comes last, never first. If a specific item is blocked ONLY on
   an owner step (credential rotation, a real payment, a key the owner must supply), finish everything else first and report
   READY with that owner step listed as the final gate — do not let one owner step block the other 296 items.

HOW TO FIX — ROOT CAUSE (verified in code, ac27255, re-checked against 66cd0cd): the provider is rate-limited
upstream (HTTP 429 — confirmed live by Round 10, this chat's P0 round and the parallel round) and the retry budget is
too small to absorb it. _provider_request (ai_engine.py:641) makes EXACTLY 2 attempts, then raises RuntimeError("AI
service returned an empty or invalid response."). 66cd0cd improved the delay to Retry-After-aware exponential backoff (1 s
/ 2 s, honouring the provider's Retry-After header) but NOT the count — it is STILL only 2 attempts. translate_pdf_layout
(ai_engine.py:1775) translates text-block batches IN PARALLEL (batch size 8, 3 workers) and ANY batch raising kills the
WHOLE translation — the blocks_preserved/fallback logic is never reached because the error propagates. FIX: (1) raise
the retry budget for translation batches to 3 attempts with exponential backoff (0.4 s, 2 s, 5 s) and retry on HTTP 429
explicitly; (2) on a batch that still fails, preserve those blocks (mark them blocks_preserved with a warning) instead of
failing the whole document; (3) log which batch/block ids failed so the failure is diagnosable; (4) surface the real provider
status (429 vs empty response) in the error message instead of the generic "Translation failed. Please retry."; (5) add a
fallback provider/key in the AI Settings gateway (owner question: is the provider on a free/rate-limited tier?). VERIFY: run
Translate EN→ES and EN→HI on test-text-10page.pdf in TWO consecutive controlled runs; expect a genuine translated
PDF both times, both languages, with the text layer checked by PyMuPDF. ID 271 (mojibake) is fixed separately: embed a
Devanagari-capable font with a correct ToUnicode CMap for non-Latin target scripts.

HOW TO FIX — ROOT CAUSE (verified in code, ac27255, re-checked against 66cd0cd): summarize_pdf
(ai_engine.py:1114) makes 4 sequential provider calls for a 69-page document — 3 chunk calls (pages 1-24, 25-48, 49-69,
chunk_size 24) + 1 final synthesis call — each with a 55 s timeout (SUMMARY_CALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 55.0)
inside a 275 s total budget (DOCUMENT_AI_BUDGET_SECONDS). Each call goes through _provider_request, which
retries only TWICE; if ANY one of the 4 calls fails twice with an empty/invalid response, the whole brief fails with "AI service
returned an empty or invalid response." (ai_engine.py:739) — the exact error seen in all 10 rounds. summarize_pdf has
NO retry and NO fallback for a failed chunk (it raises RuntimeError on an empty note). FIX: (1) raise the retry budget for
chunk calls to 3 attempts with backoff (0.4 s, 2 s, 5 s); (2) reduce chunk_size from 24 to 12 pages (smaller requests fail
less); (3) log WHICH chunk (page range) failed; (4) on a chunk failure, fall back to the offline RAG extractive note for that
chunk (_offline_rag_fallback already exists, ai_engine.py:820) instead of failing the whole brief; (5) raise the per-call
timeout for the final synthesis. VERIFY: run Brief on notes_test.pdf (69 pages, 5.2 MB) twice; expect a real brief with
verified page references both times. Also re-run the 10-page brief to confirm no regression. NOTE: 66cd0cd improved the

retry backoff (Retry-After-aware exponential) but it is STILL only 2 attempts per call — the 3rd attempt, chunk_size 24 →
12 and the per-chunk offline fallback are still needed.

| 292 | Split returns WRONG pages on ~5 MB files (silent data corruption) | Tools | Master Audit Round 11 (15 Nov 2026,
66cd0cd, F-03, HIGH): on fivemb.pdf (4 pages, 5.7 MB), range "1-2" produced a 1-page PDF whose content is literally
page 4 ("Round 11 5MB test file - page 4 of 4"); range "1" failed silently twice. The DOM contains a SECOND hidden "Page
ranges to split" input, so the handler likely reads a different field than the visible one. Page Organizer reorders the same
file correctly, so this is split-specific. Wrong-page output is the worst failure mode: the user gets a valid-looking PDF of the
wrong pages with no error. Fix: remove the duplicate input, read only the visible validated field, and add a server-side
check that output pages match the requested ranges before offering the download. [Source: Master Audit Round 11 · F-03]
